# Cross-review of the ready-to-apply core patches (cloud/core-hardening, patches-ready), queue item F2 (item F2 of `../QUEUE.md`)
Status: findings recorded and edited; NOTHING was applied, built, merged or committed (this file is a review; every fix named in it is a proposal). Money, redaction and security paths have no round cap, so a further review may follow. What was run, by whom, is in item 9 of the plain summary; every Postgres and test-behaviour statement is UNVERIFIED unless it says it was read from source.
Written 2026-11-16 09:02 UTC / 14:32 IST by the assistant (the session that runs `egm:egm-resolve`), last edited on 2026-11-16 at 15:48 UTC / 21:18 IST (batch: the fixes for the stranger-reader, design and citation reviews of the latest round; the log is `../reviews/fix-review19.md`; clock read by `date -u` at the end of the batch). Inputs: the design and codebase reviews of the three patches, `evidence-13.md` (raw output of an evidence runner), and the later reviews listed in History. Reviewer words are unverified until a row says the source was opened.

Lead default of 2026-11-16, item 4 (`../inputs/DECISIONS-2026-11-16.md`, line 5): A-01 is DEFERRED, the C-01 extras (the token ceiling) are CUT, the G5 wording patch is KEPT (text only). WHO DECIDED, plainly: the lead (the assistant-side session that directs the work), NOT the studio head in person. Line 1 of that file reads 'Lead decisions 2026-11-16 (default decisions per RIGHT-SIZE; the owner may veto any line; each is reversible)'; 'the owner' there is the studio head. So these three items are the lead's defaults: they stand until the studio head vetoes them, and I found no file showing that he confirmed any of them. These items are lead defaults, not the studio head's decision, wherever this file mentions them.

## Words and scales (defined once; every later line uses them this way)
**Who is who** (the list that follows; later lines that say 'see Who is who' mean this list):
- **Studio head**: the person who decides (the owner of Hadavin). Older text called him "the owner"; this file uses only "studio head". **The lead** is NOT him: the lead is the assistant-side session that directs the work and wrote `../inputs/DECISIONS-2026-11-16.md` (in this file `inputs/...` always means the folder `handover/flows-audit/inputs/`, `reviews/...` the folder `handover/flows-audit/reviews/`). An item of that file is a **lead default**: it stands until the studio head vetoes it (its line 1 says he may veto any line).
- **The assistant** (also "I" and "the main session" in older text): the assistant session that edits this file. **flows-audit** is the name of the audit folder it works in. **Reviewer**: a separate assistant run that reads the file or the source and reports; its words stay unverified until a row says the source was opened.
- **Evidence runner**: a separate run that executes tests and saves raw output; it never judges. **Heavy lane**: the full-repository test run described in `../heavy-test-lane/SUMMARY.md`.
- **READ THIS FIRST (terms used before they are explained)**: 'hold' = the rule that no proposal is sent and no pilot is started (sentence below); 'criterion (n)' = an exit condition of the hold, listed under Decision 1; 'login' = a database account; 'drain' = moving waiting audit events into the chain; 'signed subtree' = the files whose hash is signed (Decision 27); 'acceptance note' = a signed text that accepts one open risk (Decisions 26 to 28). Each is defined in full where it is first needed and in the words below.
- **Words with ONE meaning each (every later line follows them)**:
  - **owner** alone is never used for a person. The person who decides is the **studio head**; the person or session who writes the change is the **builder**; the person who sets up separate programs and secret stores at a client or on the studio's machines is the **deployer**; the database account that created the tables is the **migration owner** (Postgres calls it the table owner); the **database owner** is the Postgres administrator account that may change database-wide settings. Three more uses of 'owner' are technical terms with their own noun and are never a person: the **code owner** (the name the CODEOWNERS file gives to the person or team whose review a change needs), the **folder owner** (the file-system account that owns a folder) and the **table owner** (the migration owner above). The word 'owner' in the first line of `inputs/DECISIONS-2026-11-16.md` ('the owner may veto any line') means the studio head.
  - **Who is the builder and who is the deployer for this studio (which party fills which job)**: the BUILDER is an assistant build session (the repository rules say Claude does the building, `CLAUDE.md`, first section); the lead names which session. The DEPLOYER is NOT named by any file I read (I searched `handover/` and `.claude/`: the word appears only in this file and in two reviewer notes). NEEDS THE STUDIO HEAD: name the deployer (Decision 25). Readings, not facts: on the studio's own machines the only person the repository describes is the studio head (a one-person studio); at a client it may be a client person. The deployer's work is in Decisions 3, 5, 7, 9, 10, 15 and 21; Decision 25 asks who the deployer is.
  - **DSN** (database connection string): the one line that tells a program which database to reach, with which login and which secret. This file writes 'connection string' for it; the short form stays only inside setting and source names (for example `purge_dsn_secret_name`).
  - **Dates and times**: a date written alone means that UTC day. A UTC day starts at 05:30 IST on the same date and ends at 05:30 IST on the next date, so 'by 2026-11-19 UTC' means by 05:30 IST on 2026-11-20. The blocks in the decisions section write both forms.
  - **live** means a pilot or production environment only (`is_live`, `config.py:394`). Older uses (a live defect, a live Major, a live database, a live-kind component, a live-profile check) now read: present in today's code; a database that is already in use; a model kind other than `fake`; the start-up check for a pilot or production environment.
  - **reconcile** names two different things. The **approvals step `reconcile`** is the tick step that runs approved requests (`runtime/tick.py:85`). The **drain** is the method `reconcile()` of an audit sink that empties the outbox (`audit/chain.py:232`). This file writes 'the approvals step `reconcile`' for the first and 'the drain' or 'audit `reconcile()`' for the second. **Drain has ONE meaning**: the act of moving waiting events from the outbox into the chain by the audit `reconcile()` and then deleting each moved row from the outbox (`audit/chain.py:232-246`, read: the chain append, then `DELETE FROM audit_outbox` at `:244`), and nothing else. WHERE the act runs is named apart: the **tick drain step** is a step of the tick that does it (Decision 2 (a); it runs under the login the tick shares today); the **drain program** is a separate program that does only the drain, under its own login (Decision 3). Once Decision 3 is built the tick drain step is switched off in the agent and tick process, because a process that still drains keeps the right to DELETE outbox rows; so Decision 2 (a) is an interim set-up and Decision 3 is the end state. 'Drain' alone always means the act; a step is always called 'the tick drain step' and a program 'the drain program'.
  - **step** means one named part of the tick, and nothing else. Older 'FIRST STEP' and 'last step' now read 'first piece of work'. the steps of the tick are `sweep`, the approvals step `reconcile`, `stuck`, `checkpoint`, `purge_evidence`, `reprobe_sources` and, only if A-01 returns, `audit_reconcile`.
  - **login** means one database account plus its secret, and nothing else. **Identity** is the wider word: any account that can act, whether or not it is a database login (breakglass, CI deploy and humans are identities, not logins). The table of proposed logins is called the **login table** (older text and History call it 'the identity table'); it lists logins only. **Role** is Postgres's word for a login (setting names such as `runtime_role`); this file means the same thing by it and never means a group of logins.
  - **operator** written plain means the PERSON who runs a deployment day to day (also the name of one human job, 'operator', in the least-privilege rule). The proposed database login of the stop-and-reset command is always written `operator` (code font) and called 'the `operator` login' or 'the `operator` process'. They are different things: the person runs a command that connects as the login.
  - **write function** means code that writes to the store or the audit trail; **write path** means a database right plus the route by which something can be written that today's grants and code do not already allow. A new caller of an existing write function is a new write function in a patch's text (under the least-privilege rule it needs a login, a reason and a test) but not a new write path (A-01, X-2).
  - **History** (the last big section) keeps old words as written ('live', 'the lead decides', 'step', 'reconcile'); every section before it follows the definitions above. **History also keeps old numbers** (for example '29 answers', '25 numbered decisions', 'five Majors', 'fix-review16'): they are the numbers of their own round and are NOT today's; today's numbers are in 'Plain summary', 'Counts (by script)' and 'Decisions for the studio head'.
  - **Where a file named without a path sits**: a bare name such as `evidence-13.md`, `fix-review13.md`, `fix-review15.md`, `fix-patchcopies1.md` or `review-design-r17.md` is in THIS folder (`reviews-of-core-patches/`); the newer logs and reviews (`fix-review16.md` onward, `fix-patchcopies4.md`, the later reviews) are in `handover/flows-audit/reviews/`, written `../reviews/...`. A bare name that is not in this folder is a mistake to report.
  - **`/tmp` paths**: `/tmp/ch`, `/tmp/core-b4p`, `/tmp/core-b4m` and `/tmp/smfull` are scratch folders of earlier rounds. They may be cleared at any time (they existed when last checked, which proves nothing about later), so a cite into them may stop opening; a statement that rests only on such a cite was read once and cannot be re-checked later. The copies in `patch-copies/` and the evidence files in this folder are the durable record.
  - **`app.build`** is the function (`app.py:62`) that assembles a running deployment from config: it builds the store, the audit kind, the model kind and the secrets kind. **Protocol** (capital P) is a Python interface, a list of methods a kind must provide (for example `AuditSink`, `audit/protocol.py`). **Route-config condition**: lead default 4 of 2026-11-16 keeps the price check only if the take's model program reads model prices from a config file (the `Config` class that holds the `Route` objects) instead of building them in code. **ONE STATEMENT of what Q1 does (every other mention of the Route-config condition points here)**: the condition is NOT known to hold, because nobody has opened the take's model program (Q1 asks). Until Q1 is answered 'yes' or 'no', C-01 stays 'do not apply now' and Decision 18 is not asked. Q1 YES: the check inside `Config` reaches the take; Decision 18 then chooses between price-only inside `Config`, price-only where cost is computed (recommended, C-3) and dropping the patch. Q1 NO (the program builds `Route(...)` in code): the check inside `Config` never sees the take, so the patch is NOT applied as written; this answer alone does not drop the price check (the take's spend limits need it where cost is computed): Decision 18 then chooses between placing the check where cost is computed (C-3) and dropping the patch. Q1 'I don't know' counts as no answer. The condition does not change any rating (C-3 and C-7 stay Minor; C-7 never depends on it). **The take's model program** is the program or flow node (one box in a flow, or one script) that makes the model call in the recorded take; which file that is was not read (see 'Question Q1 to confirm'). **Scratch database**: a throw-away Postgres database made only for one test run, holding made-up data only, provided by the builder (a local container is allowed by the studio rule on local runners); it costs no money and proves nothing about a client's own database.

Severity (ONE scale, four levels; the word Blocker is used only as defined here):
- **Blocker** PLAIN VERSION: no patch adds a serious defect; nine serious defects exist in today's code; none of them carries the label Blocker, and nothing about the hold sentence changes whichever way Decision 20 is answered, because the hold already stops the proposal and the pilot. Decision 20 is a question about a word and a count, not about the work. DETAIL: (a test; two yes answers make a Blocker; the answers are given per patch, with cited lines, right after question (2); rows of one patch share an answer): (1) is the defect ADDED by a change this file lets someone apply (a patch or a proposed fix), so that it is absent from today's code? (2) does that change, once applied, add a right (a database grant), a write path (defined above), a loss or a way around a limit that today's code does not have? Applied, by patch (cites opened by me, nothing run): C-01: (1) no defect added except the new refusal of a zero price, which is the purpose of the patch (C-8; `C-01.patch` hunk 2, lines 14-30, the `raise` at line 27), (2) no right, no write path, no loss; A-01: (1) yes for the gaps recorded as X-2 (no login named, no reason, no test), (2) no: the step calls the existing audit `reconcile()` (`audit/chain.py:232-246`) under grants the login already holds (`store/migrate.py:57`, `:59`), so it is a new write function over an existing write path, not a new write path; G5-wording: (1) no and (2) no (comments and docstrings only); so no patch row is a Blocker. X-5 and X-6, X-6.1 to X-6.3 included, answer no to question (1): they exist in today's code and no patch causes them, so they are rated Major and are stopped by the hold sentence, not by this word. HEADLINE (always quote both halves; never write 'no Blocker' alone): 0 patch Blockers, and 9 present Blocker-equivalent parts. The 9 are the Major parts of today's code that break the least-privilege rule: X-5.1, X-5.2, X-5.3, X-6.1, X-6.2, X-6.3, X-6.4, X-6.6 and X-6.8 (the three Major departures X-6.6a to X-6.6c sit inside X-6.6; X-6.6d is a fourth departure rated Minor, so it is not one of the 9); they live inside the two counted rows X-5 and X-6, and X-4 (a lost record, no right broken) is Major but not one of the 9. WHY THE WORD IS NOT ON THEM: the test above ranks a defect by who ADDED it (question 1), not by how much it hurts, so a defect that no patch introduced can never be a Blocker under it; that is a choice of the assistant (this file's own rule, and the default 'keep' of Decision 20 is the same assistant's own recommendation), not a decision of the studio head. Whether defects of today's code of this size are called Blocker is Decision 20, option 5, and ONLY THE STUDIO HEAD decides it. Until he answers, no row carries the label Blocker, and the hold sentence below is the Blocker-equivalent for selling: it stops the proposal and the pilot the way a Blocker would stop an apply, and it ends only on the exit criteria of Decision 1.
- **Major**: see the rating rule below.
- **Minor**: a gap or a missing guard, test, log line or note; nothing shown is lost or bypassed.
- **Nit**: wording, style or a tiny cleanup.
- **Hold sentence** (replaces the earlier use of the word Blocker for X-5 and X-6): do not send the sales proposal (the written offer to a client) and do not start a pilot (the first paid trial on a client's real data) until every exit criterion listed in Decision 1 is met (today the one shared database login, rows X-5 and X-6, is the main reason). It is this file's Blocker-equivalent. THE HOLD HAS NO MECHANISM TODAY, said plainly: nothing in the code, in CI or in the repository refuses a pilot configuration or stops a proposal from being sent; it is kept only because the studio head and the assistant do not send it. Four criteria (3, 4, 5, 7) can end on an acceptance note alone and two more (2 and 8) only in part ((2): its X-6.4 part; (8): the departures, while its start-up check must be built); (1), (6) and (9) cannot. An acceptance note is a file that does not exist yet (its folder is not created), and no check reads it. PROPOSAL (not built, nothing sized: size unknown, nobody has estimated it): one start-up refusal for EVERY open criterion, not only the departures of criterion (8): a pilot or production configuration must name, for each criterion still open, an existing acceptance-note file, and for each criterion claimed met, an evidence file that names the program being started (its signed subtree hash, defined in Decision 27) and records a pass (exit code 0, every guard test green); every note must be bound to the studio head (a commit signed by a key only he holds, or a notes folder under CODEOWNERS with him as the only code owner; see criterion (8)); start-up refuses when one is missing or does not match. It cannot judge the content of a note (the studio head reads it). Until it exists the hold is a promise, not a control. The hold also has no end date: Decision 7 has only a rough code estimate (its deployer part cannot be sized until Decision 25 names the deployer), Decisions 3 and 5 have estimates, and Decision 8 has no figure (it matters for the date only if a note is refused for criterion (7)), so nobody can yet say when the criteria can be met. FIRST DATED ACTIONS (proposal; the lead to confirm; nobody is assigned yet; they are the first pieces of work on the path, NOT the whole path): the builder (a session the lead names) starts on 2026-11-17 UTC and by the end of 2026-11-19 UTC (05:30 IST on 2026-11-20) reports (a) a measured range for the code part of Decision 7, the first link of the path, and (b) the Decision 8 move tried on a scratch branch with the list of tests that break, which sizes criterion (7); criterion (7) may end on a note, so (b) is on the path only if the studio head refuses a note for (7). THE PATH that ends the hold runs through the criteria that cannot end on a note: Decision 7 (code part) and, if the studio head wants it before the pilot (Decision 29), the setting 'this process does not sign' first, then Decisions 3 and 10 (with 11), Decision 12 (with 13) and Decision 14 option A, and last the criterion (9) checks for each login once that login exists. With the two figures and the sizes in the table under 'Decisions for the studio head' the assistant writes one dated range for criteria (1) to (9) by the end of 2026-11-20 UTC (05:30 IST on 2026-11-21); that range is a document the assistant writes, NOT an end date of the hold, and for criteria (1), (6) and (8) it can only say 'unknown' until Decision 25 is answered. THE HOLD HAS NO END DATE until that range exists and Decision 25 is answered (the earlier sentence 'the hold also has no end date' says the same). The deployer part of Decision 7 cannot be sized until the studio head names the deployer (see 'Who is the builder and who is the deployer'). Demos on made-up data are not held. Whether the hold stays, whether it is split into a proposal hold and a pilot hold, and when it ends, is Decision 1. Words: a pilot is the first paid trial on a client's real data; production is a client relying on the system day to day; the setting `is_live` covers both.

Rating rule for Major (applied to every row, written once here):
A row is Major only if it is (a) a defect SHOWN by a cited source line or a run, which loses a record or lets someone get around a money limit, an approval, the emergency stop or tamper evidence, in today's code or in the text of a patch someone may apply; or (b) a broken written studio rule (the least-privilege rule, `.claude/rules/least-privilege-hard-rule.md`; earlier text called it "hard rule 11"). A risk that needs a producer or sink nobody showed, a missing test, or a missing log line is Minor at most, EXCEPT a missing item that the least-privilege rule itself demands for a new write function (one login, a per-action reason, a negative test): that is part (b) and can be Major (X-2). A read-only note that only proposes an identity (D-3) adds no write function and is Minor as a text fix; but the least-privilege rule (item 5) still asks an over-breadth review for every NEW login, and `effective_problems` is not called for any new login today (see 'Over-breadth checks still missing'), so that login is not yet checked by anything: the review is a condition of creating it, not something the Minor rating waives. Sub-point letters (a), (b), (i) inside a row belong to that row; "rule part (a)" or "rule part (b)" always names this rating rule. A patch being deferred, or a condition being open, does not change the rating; it only decides whether the row is counted as PRESENT (in today's code). Each Major row says what is SHOWN and what is NOT shown.
ONE COUNTING GRAIN: the counted row (one table row whose first cell is an id C-n, A-n, G-n, D-n or X-n; the script counts nothing else). The headline '4 Major' is 4 rows. The parts X-5.1 to X-6.9 and the departures X-6.6a to X-6.6d are rated and shown inside their row and are never added to the count. If someone counts parts instead, the same file holds 9 Major parts (X-5: 3, X-6: 6) plus the Major rows X-4 and X-2, which is 11 Major items; never quote 4 and 11 side by side. HOW THE THREE NUMBERS FIT: 4 = the counted rows X-2, X-4, X-5 and X-6 (X-2 only if A-01 returns); 9 = the Major parts inside X-5 (3) and X-6 (6); 11 = those 9 parts plus the rows X-4 and X-2.
Applied evenly, the 4 Majors are 3 present and 1 conditional (counted rows; X-5 and X-6 are rated again part by part, see their tables):
- PRESENT Major (3): X-4 (part a: nothing drains the outbox and a record written while the database is down is lost at exit), X-5 (Major parts X-5.1, X-5.2, X-5.3; Minor parts X-5.4, X-5.5; X-5.6 is a work list, not rated), X-6 (Major parts X-6.1, X-6.2, X-6.3, X-6.4, X-6.6, X-6.8; Minor parts X-6.5, X-6.7, X-6.9). They exist in today's code and need no patch. The script counts the row once; the parts are not counted.
- CONDITIONAL Major (1): X-2 (part b: the A-01 step is a new write function, and the least-privilege rule asks for one login, a per-action reason and a negative test for each write function; the patch names none; counted only if A-01 returns).
- C-3 and C-7 are Minor by the same rule: each needs something nobody showed (C-3: a route priced zero; C-7: a negative token count). C-3 was Major in earlier text and is re-rated here by the same rule as C-7. Plainly: the earlier text said 'lowered so the rows agree', and agreement between two rows is consistency, not evidence that either rating is right; the evidence for Minor is only that both need something nobody showed (a route priced zero; a negative count). A reader who thinks a money limit that can be switched off by one price of 0 is Major may rate both Major: that is option 6 of Decision 20 (6 Major, 11 Minor, 11 Nit) and is NOT the T2 choice; T2 makes only C-7 Major (5 Major, see the T2 bullet below). The placement flaw in C-3 stays a real finding with a proposed fix (see C-3).
- Two threat models, stated once: T1 (assumed for X-4 to X-6): code under the runtime login is hostile or hijacked (a hijacked agent, a hostile client hook, a stolen connection string, an injected statement). T2 (NOT assumed in any rating): a hostile model endpoint or a tampered recording file that feeds false prices or token counts. If the studio head adds T2, C-7 becomes Major (a producer then exists) and C-3 stays Minor, because the price of a route comes from the config file, which T2 does not control, and a recording supplies token counts only (`llm/fake.py:48-54`, read): 5 Major, 12 Minor, 11 Nit.
- If the studio head prefers "conditional means Minor", X-2 becomes Minor and the count is 3 Major, 14 Minor, 11 Nit (Decision 20).
- A-2, A-3 and A-6 are Minor: delay or a hidden backlog, no loss shown. The per-row guard of A-6 is still a precondition of applying A-01 (not of C-01).

Decision words in the tables: PROPOSE FIX = a change is proposed (nothing built); DECLINE = no change proposed; CONTEST and ESCALATE are allowed by the count script and unused here. "fix" in the index means PROPOSE FIX.

Project terms (one meaning each):
- **Tick**: the scheduled job (`runtime/tick.py`) that runs housekeeping steps in a loop (`sweep`, `reconcile`, `stuck`, `checkpoint`, `purge_evidence`, `reprobe_sources`). **Step**: defined in 'Words and scales' (one named part of the tick, nothing else).
- **Kind**: a swappable implementation of one capability (store, audit, model, secrets, channel), chosen in config; the studio's no-single-dependency rule requires every outside thing to sit behind one.
- **Audit sink** (**sink**): the audit writer a kind provides (the chain kind `ChainAudit` or the local-directory kind `LocalDirSink`). **Chain**: the audit table whose rows are hash-linked and cannot be rewritten by the runtime login. **Outbox**: the table (or folder) where an audit event waits when the chain write fails; older text also said "audit waiting list", always the same thing. **Drain**: defined once in 'Words and scales' (move waiting events from the outbox into the chain with `reconcile()`, then delete each moved row from the outbox).
- **Checkpoint**: a signed note of the chain's head (a row of `audit_checkpoints`). **Signer**: the credential that signs a checkpoint. **Anchor**: an outside place where the signed head is also stored, so a later rewrite can be detected.
- **Login**: defined in 'Words and scales' (one database account plus its secret). **Runtime role** (`runtime_role`): the one login the agent, the tick and the approval receiver share today. **Receiver**: the program part that takes an approver's click (`approvals/receiver.py`). **Kill switch**: the stored on/off row that stops agent runs (the emergency stop).
- **Hooks**: the functions a client supplies to the agent loop: `request_approval`, `execute`, `verify`, `rollback`. **ALLOW-class plan**: a plan the policy lets run without an approval; the agent loop executes it directly (`runtime/agent_loop.py:768`).
- **Route**: the config object for one model route (model id, input and output price per million tokens, `max_tokens`; `config.py:64`). **Config**: the whole validated config (`class Config`, `config.py`). **Notional price**: a stand-in price per million tokens that an operator sets for a self-hosted model, so spend limits still fire (C-8). **Choke point**: the one place every caller passes, so one check there covers all callers.
- **K1b**: the command-line replay and evaluation tools in `src/hadavin_sdk/k1b/`, which write audit events from short-lived commands. **WARDEN**: the outside repository the guard code was ported from (`tests/warden_port/`).
- **G5 and G2**: rules of the outbound text gate in `approvals/gate.py` (G5: an independent second scan; G2: unverifiable claims). They are not reviewer labels. **C-01, A-01, G5-wording**: the three patch files. **C-1, A-1, ...**: findings (rows) about them. **X-n**: findings about today's code.
- **The take**: the one recorded real run for the video (lead default 1 of 2026-11-16: scenes 1 to 4 of the run sheet only). **The video path**: the pieces of the program that run in that take.
- **live**: defined in 'Words and scales' (a pilot or production environment, `is_live`, `config.py:394`) and used only so; "PRESENT" means "exists in today's code"; **fake**: the stand-in model kind named `fake`, allowed only outside live; **guard test**: a repository test that scans files (the vendor-name guard, for example); **database guard**: a proposed trigger or constraint (X-6); **code check**: a refusal written in code (for example the price check); **verification**: a test, script run or source reading that can fail ("checked" in this file means verified by reading unless it says run); **cut**: removed from scope by a lead default (the studio head may veto it); **cap/limit/ceiling**: a spend limit is a cap, the `max_tokens` number is a ceiling.
- **More terms**: **Queue item F2**: the entry for this review in `../QUEUE.md`. **Heavy lane (H1)**: the full-repository test run kept in `../heavy-test-lane/` (H1 is its label). **`tick_state`**: the table that records, for each tick step, when it last ran (`last_run_at`), last succeeded (`last_ok_at`) and its last error text. **Portal**: the read-only view people use to see runs; its database login is `portal_role`. **Dead-man check**: a query run by an operator that raises an alert when a tick step has not succeeded for too long (the alarm that fires when the regular sign of life stops; `DEAD-MAN-CHECK.md`). **Plan**: what the agent proposes to do (table `plans`). **PENDING and APPROVED**: states of an approval request: PENDING waits for a person, APPROVED means a person said yes (the others are REJECTED and EXPIRED). **Redaction map**: the stored table `redaction_maps` that links placeholders back to the real original values of identifier kinds (the plaintext originals). **Restored values**: the plan's inputs after placeholders are replaced by the originals, just before `execute`. **`gate.decide` record**: the audit event the approval gate writes when it decides; it carries the **approved digest**, a fingerprint of the restored values at approval time, which `execute` compares again before it runs (`agent_loop.py:845-867`). **RUNG table** (the patch README's 'RUNG lines'): the table the studio's code-last rule asks for, naming for each part which cheaper option was tried first. **Words with two senses, fixed here**: "client" means the customer of Hadavin; code that calls the library is "caller code" and "client hooks" are the functions the customer supplies. "README" alone means the repository README; the patch's own file is always "the patch README". "decision" (lower case, a table column) is the verdict word PROPOSE FIX or DECLINE; "Decision N" is a numbered choice for the studio head; "lead default 4 of 2026-11-16" is an item in `inputs/DECISIONS-2026-11-16.md`, written by the lead. "login" and "role" mean one thing (a database account plus its secret; "identity" is the wider word, defined in 'Words and scales'); `runtime_role`, `portal_role` and `purge_role` are the setting names of three logins.
- **UNVERIFIED**: not run and not checked against a real database; read from source or from reviewer knowledge only. **src**: a line of this checkout, opened by me. **Patched copy**: a line of `/tmp/core-b4p` (commit b4cce5b plus the patches; a scratch folder that may be cleared, see '`/tmp` paths' in 'Words and scales'). **(a), (b), (i)**: sub-points inside one row.

## Plain summary (read this first)
1. What this is: a review of three ready-made changes ("patches") for the Hadavin core code library, of one documentation note (DEAD-MAN-CHECK.md), and of problems in today's code that no patch touches. The patches are C-01 (refuses a model price of zero or less), A-01 (a tick step that empties the outbox, the table where audit events wait) and G5-wording (comment and text changes only). Rows named C-3, X-5 and so on are the findings, listed in the index below. Words are defined in 'Words and scales' above.
2. Nothing was applied, built, merged or committed. Every fix in this file is a proposal.
3. C-01: DO NOT APPLY NOW. A price-only file now exists: `patch-copies/C-01-price-only.patch` (78 lines; two files, the `config.py` hunk `@@ -362,6 +362,16 @@` and the test file `@@ -0,0 +1,51 @@`; the price rule only, no `max_tokens` ceiling, no `defaults.py` hunk). `patch-copies/` now holds three patch files (A-01, C-01, C-01-price-only); the two C-01 files cannot be applied together and `patch-copies/README.md` says which to use (the price-only one). The README records +30 tests for the price-only form and a pass rule of clauses (a) to (d) (the section 'Pass rule' of `patch-copies/README.md`; clause (d) is the one for the two guard tests that are already red; there is no clause (e), and an earlier line of this file that said '(a) to (e)' was wrong) (the same README gives 9394 = 9364 + 30 for a full run, in its section 'Results of the fifth pass'; I read it and did not re-run it; that README is still being edited, so this file cites its quoted text and its section names, never its line numbers); the README also states 12 failed and 18 passed on the old code for the two new test files, which I did not re-run. The opening status block of `patch-copies/README.md` (its bullet 'Price check (C-01)') says: price-only 24 new tests plus 6 of A-01 = 30 in the recipe; `C-01.patch` has 26 new tests, 32 with A-01. (An earlier reading of that block as '24 instead of 26, which disagrees with +30' was wrong: there is no contradiction.) I counted the cases in the patch text (read, pytest not run): the price-only test file has 6 + 1 + 16 + 1 = 24 cases (two parametrized price tests, one single test, the non-finite test with 2 x 2 x 4 values, the fake test), `C-01.patch` has 2 more (the two ceiling tests) = 26, and `A-01.patch` has 6 test functions; so 24 + 6 = 30 and 26 + 6 = 32 agree. The earlier sentence that no price-only file exists, and any older figure of +32 tests or a four-file set for the price-only form, does not occur in this file. `C-01.patch` as written still carries the token ceiling (cut by the lead's default) and does not apply to this checkout. The file as written carries the token ceiling (cut by the lead's default) and does not apply to this checkout. Keep only the price check, and only under the Route-config condition: Q1 (which program makes the model call in the take) is not answered, so nothing is applied and Decision 18 is not yet asked (the ONE STATEMENT in 'Words and scales' says what each Q1 answer allows; where the check lives is C-3 and Decision 18). It refuses a model priced at zero in every environment where the model kind is not `fake`, and `fake` is refused in live, so in live nothing is exempt; a self-hosted model needs a positive stand-in price (C-8, Decision 19).
4. A-01: DEFERRED by the lead's default of 2026-11-16, item 4 (the studio head may veto it). No action now. If it returns, fix X-2 (which login runs the new step) and the per-row guard A-6 first. Deferring it leaves X-4 present: nothing in this repository ever drains the outbox. A-01 would narrow X-4 but would NOT close the case where the database is down (that case needs a durable fallback that does not use the database; only an outline exists, in Decision 2, unreviewed and unbuilt).
5. G5-wording: apply after one wording fix (G-1) and a small rebase, because one of its three files no longer applies to this checkout.
6. DEAD-MAN-CHECK.md (a note that tells an operator how to check that the tick steps keep running): keep it as a note and apply the one edit list under 'Edit list for DEAD-MAN-CHECK.md' (while A-01 is deferred it names four steps, not five, and it must say that nothing checks the outbox).
7. Three serious problems exist in today's code and need no patch (no patch causes them; A-01, if it returned, would narrow X-4 but not close it). X-4: nothing drains the outbox, and an audit record written while the database is down is lost when the program exits. X-5: the database login the agent runs under can delete, forge, plant and block audit records. X-6: that same login can switch off the emergency stop, set its spend counters to zero and approve its own requests, and it can read the stored original values behind the placeholders (X-6.8). Fixing X-6 starts with per-process secret scoping (each process can read only its own secret; not in this repository): size large, and the work is split three ways: the studio head decides (Decision 7), the builder (our code) builds the scoping and the start-up refusal, and the deployer sets up separate processes and secret mounts. Without it a split into several logins protects nothing.
8. Who can use that login: any code that runs under it. That is the client's `execute`, `verify` and `rollback` hooks (the tick calls them at `runtime/agent_loop.py:886`, `:901`, `:909`), the ALLOW-class plans the agent loop executes itself (`agent_loop.py:768`), anyone who steals the connection string, and any injected database statement. The model itself holds no SQL as far as the files read show (not searched exhaustively). Every deployment built by `app.build` uses one login for agent, tick and receiver: it builds one store (`app.py:146`), and the only second login setting is `purge_dsn_secret_name` (`app.py:86-89`, `:151-160`); so for a deployment built by `app.build` the one-login fact is settled by source, not by a run (a deployment that runs the agent, tick and receiver as separate programs was not checked). Hold sentence: do not send the proposal or start a pilot until the exit criteria of Decision 1 are met; it is this file's Blocker-equivalent.

## Index: one line per row
Words used: "fix" means the row proposes a change; "decline" means no change is proposed. Rating is the table value (rating rule above). Blocker: none. Rows of the same patch are together. DEAD-MAN-CHECK.md is a documented check (no code): keep it as a note and apply the one edit list under 'Edit list for DEAD-MAN-CHECK.md'.
- Patch C-01 (price check):
  - C-1 (Nit, decline): the worry that a not-a-number price breaks config loading does not occur; the pinned library already refuses it.
  - C-2 (Nit, fix): the token ceiling of 200,000 is a guess; moot while the studio head keeps the ceiling cut.
  - C-3 (Minor, fix; was Major, re-rated by the one rule): the price check sits where the demo's own model settings never pass; one placement where cost is computed and charged, one change with C-7.
  - C-4 (Nit, fix): the replay code already refuses a zero price; keep one source of truth; a tiny positive price still passes.
  - C-5 (Nit, decline): the test fixture and error-text assumptions hold.
  - C-6 (Nit, fix): add a README note that a zero price now refuses to start.
  - C-7 (Minor, fix): a negative token count or amount would lower spend; a missing guard, no producer found; one change with C-3.
  - C-8 (Minor, fix): a self-hosted model priced at zero is refused too; only the fake kind is exempt; give it a stand-in price.
- Patch A-01 (outbox step; DEFERRED, no action now; if it returns fix X-2 and A-6 first):
  - A-1 (Minor, fix): the docstring says events held in memory are drained, but memory belongs to one program.
  - A-2 (Minor, fix): the step skips a sink without `reconcile` instead of turning red; also `K1bAudit` has no `reconcile()` although its docstring promises one (the false promise is recorded under X-4).
  - A-3 (Minor, fix): a stuck backlog logs the same line as an empty one.
  - A-4 (Minor, fix): neither audit sink logs a failed last resort today; the patch covers only one.
  - A-5 (Minor, fix): no test seeds a waiting row and shows the tick drains it.
  - A-6 (Minor, fix): one bad waiting row blocks every row after it; the per-row guard is a precondition of applying A-01.
  - A-7 (Nit, fix): the step name `audit_reconcile` looks like the approvals step `reconcile`.
  - A-8 (Nit, fix): the README should name the extra docstring and test edits.
  - A-9 (Nit, decline): direct assignment in tests is safe.
- Patch G5-wording (text only):
  - G-1 (Minor, fix): the new header says nothing re-scans text; the approval channel does one final redaction pass.
  - G-2 (Nit, fix): state which tree each G5 line number refers to; one history comment in `redact.py`.
- DEAD-MAN-CHECK.md (a documented check; instruction: keep it as a note and apply the one edit list below; while A-01 is deferred it names four steps):
  - D-1 (Nit, fix): remove a sentence that overstates what the warning covers.
  - D-2 (Nit, fix): the SQL works on Postgres only; add a clock-skew note.
  - D-3 (Minor, fix): step list, missing read-only login, `last_error` leaving the app, placeholder syntax.
  - One edit list for the file: see 'Edit list for DEAD-MAN-CHECK.md' after the D rows.
- Problems found in today's code or missed by the patches:
  - X-1 (Minor, fix): nothing bounds max_tokens times price per call; moot while the ceiling is cut.
  - X-2 (Major, fix, only if A-01 returns): the new step writes to the audit trail; no login, reason or negative test is given.
  - X-3 (Minor, fix): two drainers at once double-count; untested.
  - X-4 (Major, fix, present today): nothing calls the audit `reconcile()`; DB-down records are lost at exit; A-01 would narrow this but not close the DB-down case.
  - X-5 (Major, fix, present today): the runtime login may delete, forge, plant and block audit records; six parts, rated by part: X-5.1 delete waiting records (Major), X-5.2 forged and planted rows (Major), X-5.3 planted checkpoint row (Major), X-5.4 held audit lock (Minor), X-5.5 unreadable row blocks reads (Minor), X-5.6 what the drain login breaks (work list, not rated).
  - X-6 (Major, fix, present today): one login for agent, tick and approval receiver; nine parts X-6.1 to X-6.9, rated by part: kill switch (Major), spend (Major), self-approval (Major), approval binding (Major), guard gaps (Minor), identities (Major, least-privilege rule), rollout (Minor), reading the stored originals (Major, least-privilege rule), approved-plan integrity (Minor, X-6.9); each says what is wrong, who can do it and what it costs.
- Other sections: 'Single-dependency (lock-in) check', 'Counts (by script)', 'README claims', 'Not checked', 'Decisions for the studio head' (29 numbered decisions, of which 9 and 23 are each asked as two parts (9a and 9b, 23a and 23b), plus the question 5-K and the question Q1: 27 + 4 + 2 = 33 answers), 'Update: full-repository run' (the full run is NOT green), 'History' (round numbers, reviewer ids and old text).

## Verdicts by patch, and problems in today's code (details)
- **Price check (C-01): DO NOT APPLY NOW.** A price-only copy now exists: `patch-copies/C-01-price-only.patch` (config.py hunk `@@ -362,6 +362,16 @@`, test hunk `@@ -0,0 +1,51 @@`, no `defaults.py` hunk, no ceiling; +30 tests per `patch-copies/README.md` ('9394 = 9364 + 30', README section 'Results of the fifth pass'; 24 price-only cases + 6 A-01 cases, counted by me from the patch text; not re-run). It was built after the text below was written, so the text below describes `C-01.patch` as first reviewed and is kept to show what the price-only file removes; the rebuilt `C-01.patch` copy also carries a parametrised `test_a_non_finite_price_is_a_clean_config_error` (match 'finite', `C-01.patch:87-93`; `C-01-price-only.patch:66-72`), which pins the refusal pydantic already makes (`fix-patchcopies1.md:9`) and so cannot fail on old code. The file has four parts: (1) the hunk in `config.py` at `Route.max_tokens` (`le=defaults.MAX_ROUTE_TOKENS`) and (2) the hunk in `defaults.py` (`MAX_ROUTE_TOKENS = 200_000`, a guessed number): both are the ceiling the lead's default cut, remove both; (3) the price check inside `Config` (config.py hunk 2): keep only as an optional early warning: the guard itself belongs where cost is computed (C-3, ONE PLACEMENT), because a `Route(...)` built directly never reaches `Config` (the demo, `fixtures.py`, `tests/contracts/kinds/builtin.py`); it does not apply to this checkout as shipped (exit 1) and needs one context line cut (`-C1`, exit 0); (4) `tests/test_c01_route_limits.py`: keep the price tests (the six price cases, `test_a_priced_live_route_still_loads`, `test_the_fake_model_may_cost_nothing`), remove the two ceiling tests (`test_max_tokens_above_the_ceiling_is_refused`, `test_max_tokens_at_the_ceiling_loads_and_zero_is_still_refused`) and the `defaults` import with them. The price-only version is kept only under the Route-config condition (Q1, not answered; the ONE STATEMENT in 'Words and scales' says what each answer does; not verified; not in the video path). Of the 13 new tests said to fail on the old code (the evidence file's reading, not re-run by me; see item 9 of the plain summary), only the 6 price tests belong to work that stays in; the other 7 belong to the deferred A-01 (5) or the cut ceiling (2). Hunk 2 also refuses a self-hosted model priced at zero (C-8).
- **Outbox step (A-01): DEFERRED by the lead's default (2026-11-16, item 4; the studio head may veto it). No action now. If it returns: fix X-2 and the per-row guard A-6 first.** Reason for A-6 first: once the step runs every tick, one stuck row delays every row after it on every tick, loudly on the chain kind and silently on the local-directory kind, though no audit event is lost. A-2 and A-3 are Minor under the rating rule, A-1 and A-5 are Minor, with reasons in their rows.
- **Wording patch (G5): apply after fixes**: one wording finding, G-1 (Minor: the new header may understate the protection that exists, its strength was not compared); its test-file hunk needs a rebase on this checkout (exit 1). DEAD-MAN-CHECK.md: keep as a note; corrections in D-1, D-2 and D-3 (text only), applied as the one edit list.
- **Problems in today's code that need no patch** (read from source, not run): X-4, X-5 and X-6, described in the plain summary (item 7) and in their rows. The database guard proposed earlier for X-6.3 must NOT be built as written (X-6.5). Nothing was built or tested.
- **A missing guard, not a defect present in today's code:** C-7. A negative token count or a negative amount would move spend down. No known source produces one; it is counted apart from the problems present in today's code, does not depend on the Route-config condition and is not part of patch C-01. G-1 exists only once the G5 patch is applied.

## What the evidence shows (evidence-13.md, raw; run on scratch trees of commit b4cce5b, so not the full repository)
- Of the 15 tests that failed on the old source in the evidence run (`evidence-13.md:23-24`: 15 failed, 55 passed), 13 are new tests of the patches (C-01: 6 price cases and 2 ceiling cases = 8; A-01: 5) and 2 are not: an existing test whose expected list changed (`test_p7_tick::test_steps_order_and_summary_line_is_not_a_step`) and a manifest test (`test_p7_tick::test_slack_app_manifest_is_least_privilege`) that also fails on the patched tree because a docs file is missing in the scratch tree, so it proves nothing about the patch. Of the 13 new tests only the 6 price cases belong to work that stays in; 7 belong to the deferred A-01 (5) or the cut ceiling (2, which fail with `AttributeError: MAX_ROUTE_TOKENS` at setup, `evidence-13.md:43-58`, not by behaviour). The failure REASON is shown in the raw output for only 3 groups: price cases (DID NOT RAISE ConfigError), the two ceiling cases (no attribute) and the step-list tests (step missing from the list; `evidence-13.md:63-73` is the A-01 list test only, the `test_p7_tick` list test is `:74-87`). By my reading of the FIRST-version `A-01.patch` (not evidence output; the rebuilt copy `patch-copies/A-01.patch` has the step-list test at `:178`, the drain test at `:182`, the missing-reconcile test at `:198` (now failing the step, not skipping), the log tests at `:210` and `:220` and a local-directory test at `:233`): the drain test and the missing-reconcile test call `tick.audit_reconcile`, which does not exist on the old code; the two log tests call `ChainAudit.write_outcome`, which exists, and fail on the old code because the log line is missing. The claim "13 new tests fail on the old code" is the evidence file's own reading (`evidence-13.md:23-24`), not re-run by me; the next bullet explains the line-count gap. `ruff check`, `ruff format --check` and `bandit -ll` return 0 on the patched tree (runner's claim). All three patches apply alone and in order on b4cce5b copies (runner's claim; I re-ran `git apply --check` there with the same result); on this checkout C-01 and G5 do not apply as shipped (see 'Inputs read, and the three line bases'). Mutation checks: removing the price check, dropping the output price, changing `le` to `lt`, removing the ceiling and removing the step from STEPS were each caught by a test.
- Gaps the runner states: the scratch trees hold only src, tests and pyproject.toml, so 22 test files under tests/demo_askdata failed to load (22 collection errors, not 22 tests: 20 because the askdata module is missing, 2 because a file is missing; evidence-13.md:13) and 5 other tests fail in BOTH trees for missing repo files (README, CHANGELOG, .gitleaks.toml, a docs manifest, no .git). A sixth failure in the old tree is a uv.lock test (the lock file exists only in the patched tree because the runner created it). So "5 failed" is the same 5 before and after, not caused by the patches; it is not a green full-repository run.
- Contradicts or adds: the A-01 tests that call the new step function directly (the drain count and the skip of a kind without reconcile; the two log tests call `write_outcome`, not the step) did NOT fail when the evidence runner removed the step from STEPS (evidence-13.md:99, mutation (c); I did not run it); only the list tests did. Those direct tests prove the function, not that the tick runs it. Note: the two list tests are exactly what caught the removal (evidence-13.md:99), so 'every test green' is not true; see A-5 for what is still untested.
- Test-file sizes: `evidence-13.md:105` gives +37 and +55 added lines for the two new test files and the hunk headers of the FIRST-version files said +1,53 and +1,74; the rebuilt copies say `@@ -0,0 +1,64 @@` (`C-01.patch:48`), `@@ -0,0 +1,123 @@` (`A-01.patch:124`) and `@@ -0,0 +1,51 @@` (`C-01-price-only.patch:27`). The difference is blank lines: I counted 53 lines with 16 blank (53-16=37) and 74 lines with 19 blank (74-19=55) in `/tmp/core-b4p/tests/` (UNVERIFIED: no saved output of the counts), and `evidence-13.md:22` says the old-source run used the BEFORE tree plus the four test files from the AFTER tree. So the evidence describes these test files. How the runner counted lines was not checked, and I did not re-run the tests.
- I checked (a) by reading, not by running, the output of an evidence runner (`evidence-pydantic-nan.md` was produced by an evidence runner, per its own header; I did not run it): the claim that NaN crashes `load_config` and Infinity passes is NOT real in this checkout. `Route` price fields are `Money = Annotated[Decimal, BeforeValidator(_money)]` (src `config.py:49-56`; the validator only refuses a float, then pydantic's `Decimal` rules apply), and pydantic 2.14.1 (the pin in pyproject.toml and uv.lock of this checkout) refuses NaN, sNaN, Infinity and -Infinity with "Input should be a finite number" (evidence-pydantic-nan.md PART 1, `Route(...)` direct: '0', '-1' and '0.0000001' ACCEPTED). `load_config` WAS run end to end in that evidence file (PART 2, lines 58-88): NaN, Infinity and -Infinity each raised `ConfigError` (`routes.default.input_usd_per_mtok` and `output_usd_per_mtok`, finite_number). NOT run through `load_config`: sNaN, '0' and '-1' (the first two only through `Route(...)`; PART 2 shows no output for them). The pydantic pin at b4cce5b is 2.14.1 (`/tmp/core-b4p/pyproject.toml:16`, read), the same as this checkout. My earlier statement about plain Decimal comparison (NaN raising `InvalidOperation`, Infinity being greater than 0) has no saved output in that evidence file, so it is not relied on. (b) `approvals/channel.py:29` runs `redact_text` as the last operation on the channel text, so the point that the new G5 header says "nothing in this repository re-scans text before it leaves", which may understate the protection that exists (the final pass exists; whether it covers the same patterns as the earlier redaction was not compared); the earlier word 'over-claims' was imprecise) is real.

## Patch C-01 (price above 0 and a ceiling on max_tokens): DO NOT APPLY NOW (price-only version after the choke-point decision in C-3; see 'Verdicts by patch')
| id | sev | merged from | decision | what to change |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C-1 | Nit | reviewer finding | DECLINE (optional hardening only; see the end of this row) | The original finding said "NaN" crashes `load_config` and "Infinity" passes. That is wrong for the pinned library: pydantic 2.14.1 already refuses all four non-finite values at the `Route` field, before the patch's `min(...) <= 0` check runs, so the patch never sees them. What stays true: the Decimal comparison would misbehave if a non-finite value ever got through (a different pydantic version or `allow_inf_nan` turned on). Optional hardening: write the check as `not (p.is_finite() and p > 0)`. The test cases for NaN and Infinity are no longer only a proposal: the rebuilt copies already carry `test_a_non_finite_price_is_a_clean_config_error` (`C-01.patch:87-93`, `C-01-price-only.patch:66-72`), which passes on old code too and so proves nothing about the patch; the `is_finite()` code is not in the copies. Run (evidence-pydantic-nan.md PART 2): `load_config` end to end with NaN, Infinity and -Infinity, all refused with `ConfigError`; not run: sNaN, '0' and '-1' through `load_config`. Reason for the downgrade: premise contradicted by the evidence runner's direct run in this checkout (`evidence-pydantic-nan.md`; I read its output and did not run it). the row itself says the defect does not occur and the tests it would add pass on the old code too, so nothing here fixes a defect; the `is_finite()` form stays as an optional note, not a finding to fix. |
| C-2 | Nit | reviewer finding | PROPOSE FIX | `MAX_ROUTE_TOKENS = 200_000` is a guess (the patch README says the number is its author's choice and needs the studio head's yes, the patch README's decision table, row 3 ('the number is my placeholder, not from a provider page')) and looks like a context size, not an output limit, so a 10x typo passes. This finding rests on limits that were NOT checked: neither the patch README text nor any provider's real output limits were supplied to or read by this review (see Not checked). Moot while the lead's default keeps the ceiling CUT. Make it a Config setting with the default in `defaults.py` (same pattern as `executor_timeout_s`, src `config.py:360-362`), and take the default from the provider's own output limits. The number is the studio head's decision (see end). Rating Nit. Reason: this row shows no defect in today's code or in the patch; it rests on a guessed number (200_000) whose provider limits nobody checked, the lead's default cut the ceiling, and the hazard it names (a ten-times typo of `max_tokens` passing) overlaps X-1 (Minor, no product bound). A Major needs a shown defect or a broken rule. If the ceiling returns, Decision 16 at the end still applies. The choice of scale is the reviewer's, recorded here. |
| C-3 | Minor | reviewer finding, re-checked | PROPOSE FIX | In plain words: the price check the patch adds sits in a place the demo's own model settings never pass through, so a zero price there would still switch off every spend limit. Rating (rule part (a), applied as for C-7): Minor (it was Major in earlier text). SHOWN (source read, confirmed by the codebase reviewer): the check is inside `Config`, and the demo builds its core `Route` from its own settings without `Config` (`demos/ask-your-data/askdata/config.py:61-70`), so the check never sees it; every price consumer multiplies by that price (the places are listed in Decision 18, 'ONE PLACEMENT'). NOT shown: a route in a pilot or production environment that really carries price 0 (no demo config file holds a price) and a failing test (none written, none run). So this is a placement flaw in the patch as written, on a money limit, not a loss; like C-7 it needs something nobody showed (a route priced zero), so it is Minor; whether the patch is kept at all depends on Q1 (the ONE STATEMENT in 'Words and scales'), and with Q1 'no' this placement flaw is the reason the check must live where cost is computed. A `backends.detector` of kind `llm_judge` does not change this: the judge runs on the configured llm kind (src `backends/table.py:545`, the comment 'llm_judge connects through the configured llm kind', and src `guard/judge.py:40`, `JudgeDetector(deps.llm, settings.route, ...)`), so a fake llm means a fake judge and the patch's condition is right for that path; `config.py:384-386` only checks that the route name exists and `config.py:157-160` only refuses a list holding nothing but llm_judge. The argument that stands is the one below: every consumer multiplies by the route price, and some `Route(...)` objects are built without `load_config`, so a check inside `Config` never sees them (the demo's `AskdataConfig.routes()`, `demos/ask-your-data/askdata/fixtures.py:24-25`, `tests/contracts/kinds/builtin.py:20`). REAL REASON (checked in src): the runtime caps multiply by the route price, so at a price of 0 they never fire. Every consumer of the price (src, all opened by me): `limits.py:17-18` `Limits.charge` adds the reply cost to the day and month spend, which the budget checks at `limits.py:20-28` read; `runtime/agent_loop.py:485-486` charges `reply.cost_usd` and adds it to the run cost, and `runtime/agent_loop.py:256-257` compares that run cost with the per-run cap `max_run_usd`; `runtime/agent_loop.py:325-326` does the same for a verdict cost; `evals.py:80` sums `reply.cost_usd` for the eval run cost; `k1b/loop.py:120-121` (the reserve for an abandoned call uses both prices) and `k1b/loop.py:170` (`budget.charge(worst_case_usd(route_prices, ...))`, under the `if route_prices is not None` at `:169`), fed by `k1b/replay.py:166` `route_prices=cfg.routes[agent.model_route]`; `llm/common.py:166-169` (the multiplication is at 167-168, the division at 169) (cost of a real call) and `llm/fake.py:51-52` (cost of a fake call). Completes the list (src, opened by me): `k1b/loop.py:179` `budget.charge(reply.cost_usd)` (the cost of each reply) and `k1b/loop.py:206` `budget.charge(verdict.cost_usd)` (the cost of each injection verdict), and `extract.py:24-25` `Valid.cost_usd`, which sums `r.cost_usd` over the replies (the root `extract.py`, not under `k1b/`); both read the reply cost, which is price times tokens, so a zero price zeroes them too. Adds two consumers of the PRICE itself in the demo (src, opened by me): `demos/ask-your-data/askdata/meter.py:48-55` `worst_case_usd` (the two multiplications by `route.input_usd_per_mtok` and `route.output_usd_per_mtok` are at `:53-54`), so a zero price gives a zero reserve and the demo cap can never be reached (called from `askdata/pipeline.py:145`); and `demos/ask-your-data/askdata/config.py:64-69` (`AskdataConfig.routes()`) builds the core `Route(...)` from the demo's own `RouteCfg` (`config.py:39-44`: plain `Decimal`, no bound), never through `load_config`. Minor downstream readers of `reply.cost_usd`: `guard/judge.py:86` and `:96`, and `guard/injection.py:77` (opened: it only sums the verdicts' `cost_usd`, no price multiplication). Other places that build a `Route(...)` without `load_config` (src, opened): `demos/ask-your-data/askdata/fixtures.py:24-25` and `tests/contracts/kinds/builtin.py:20`, so a check on `Route` itself also reaches them (fixtures with a zero price would then need the fake-kind way out). CONSEQUENCE FOR THE FIX: a check placed in `Config._cross_checks` (src `config.py:378`) would not see the demo, because the demo never builds a core `Config` for its route. ONE PLACEMENT (proposal, not built): the guard lives where cost is computed and charged, as one function (for example `require_positive_price(route, kind)`, defined once) called at the places that turn a price into a cost: `llm/common.py:166-169` (the multiplication is at 167-168, the division at 169) (real kinds: refuse a price at or below zero BEFORE the provider call), `llm/fake.py:51-52` (the fake kind: zero allowed, negative refused), the demo's own reserve `demos/ask-your-data/askdata/meter.py:48-55`, and `k1b/replay.py:85-90` (which then calls the same function, see C-4); plus `Limits.charge` (`limits.py:17-18`) refusing a negative amount. This is the same change as C-7 (one change, two rows; the earlier idea of a helper called by about twelve consumers is dropped because it is the opposite of one choke point). The `kind` is known where the llm kind runs, so `Route` does not need to know it. The patch's config-load check can stay as an optional early warning (a clear message at start); it is not the guard. Cost: a bad price is refused at the first call, not at start, unless the early warning is kept. At a price of 0 every one of these returns 0 and the per-run cap, the daily and monthly budgets and the replay `--max-usd` cap can never fire. Negative price: src `k1b/replay.py:87` (`_refuse_zero_price`) tests `== 0` only, so a negative price passes replay today; a negative price makes the spend go DOWN, so the check must be `> 0` (as the patch's `min(...) <= 0` is: read in C-01.patch hunk 2 and in `/tmp/core-b4p` `config.py:365-373`), and it needs a negative-price test case (evidence-pydantic-nan.md PART 1 shows '-1' ACCEPTED by `Route`). Refuse zero and negative prices for every route used by a component that calls a model of a kind other than `fake`. A failing test for C-3 was NOT written and NOT run (the patched tree exists at `/tmp/core-b4p`, but I did not write or run a test), so C-3 is a reading of the source, not a proved bug. Because every consumer multiplies by the route price, the check belongs once where cost is computed (ONE PLACEMENT above), not in K1b replay, which is why `k1b/replay.py:85-90` duplicating it (see C-4) should call that function. Conditional: the patch as written is kept only under the Route-config condition (lead default 2026-11-16 item 4; Q1 unanswered; unverified); the placement fix is the same in either case. ADDITIONS (src, tests and demos opened by me; nothing run). (a) moved to its own row C-7: it does not depend on the Route-config condition, so it must not be counted as conditional with the price check. (b) `model_copy` limit: a check on `Route` does not run on `model_copy(update=...)`. Grepped `src`, `tests` and `demos` for `model_copy`: the only Route copy that changes a price is `tests/test_k1b_replay.py:381-385` (`_zero_price` runs `:381-385`; the `model_copy` line is `:382`; built on purpose to test the replay refusal); the reviewer's other cites do not hold: `demos/ask-your-data/askdata/config.py:94` copies the `AskdataConfig` to set `paths`, and `askdata/evalcli.py:154` and `:185` copy the eval `lock`; none touches a Route. So the bypass is real but is used only by that test; keep the replay check (`k1b/replay.py:85-90`) as the second guard for configs that reach it without `Route(...)`, and write the new Route test with `Route(...)`, not `model_copy`. The fake-kind point holds, and is wider than the reviewer said: `_refuse_zero_price` computes `zero = input == 0 or output == 0` (`k1b/replay.py:87`) and refuses only `if zero and llm_kind != "fake"` (`:88`), so a NEGATIVE price passes replay for EVERY kind, `fake` or not. Fix: refuse a negative price always (the fake kind may keep only the zero exemption) and add a negative-price test with the fake kind and one with a kind other than `fake`. (c) Demo error path (read, not run): `AskdataConfig.routes()` (`demos/ask-your-data/askdata/config.py:61-70`) builds the core `Route` and is called from `askdata/evalcli.py:70` and `:88` inside `harness_factory`, which `cmd_run` calls at `evalcli.py:147` under `except (ConfigError, KeyError, OSError)` (`:148`); the demo's `load_config` (`config.py:80-94`; the `except` is at `:87`) does not call `routes()`, so a bad price passes `load_config` and fails later. A pydantic `ValidationError` from a Route-level check is a `ValueError` but not the `ConfigError` caught there: at `evalcli.py:148` `ConfigError` is the demo's own `askdata.config.ConfigError(ValueError)` (`demos/ask-your-data/askdata/config.py:21`, imported at `evalcli.py:24`), a sibling subclass of `ValueError`, not `hadavin_sdk.errors.ConfigError` (`errors.py:4`), so it is not caught at `:148` or at `evalcli.py:259` (which wraps `load_config` only): the demo would stop with a traceback, no `startup:` line and not exit code 4, after `run_dir.mkdir` (the sealed-set counter at `evalcli.py:154-157` moves only after startup succeeds, so it is not burned). Fix: build the routes inside the demo's `load_config` (its `except` already covers `ValidationError` and turns it into `ConfigError`), or turn the error into `ConfigError` in `routes()`. Test (none exists, the demo has no test folder of its own; tests live under `tests/demo_askdata/`): a demo config with a zero price and one with a negative price exit with `startup:` and code 4. Why Minor: a Major under rule part (a) needs a defect shown that loses a record or gets around a limit. This row shows a patch that would not protect a route built outside `Config`, but the limit is bypassed only if a route priced zero exists (none shown), the same condition that keeps C-7 Minor. The studio head may overrule it (Decision 20). |
| C-4 | Nit | reviewer finding | PROPOSE FIX | Checked: src `k1b/replay.py:85-90` (`_refuse_zero_price`) already refuses a zero price for one agent's route at replay, so the patch duplicates that rule in a second place (one source of truth: have replay call the config check, but only after the placement in C-3 is decided (one function where cost is computed); until then this item only records the duplication). A tiny positive price (for example 0.000001 per million tokens) passes both checks and still makes the cap useless: add a minimum price floor in config, or one README line stating that only zero and negative are refused. The replay check tests `== 0` (`k1b/replay.py:87`), so a NEGATIVE price passes replay for every kind (read, not run); the one shared check should refuse `<= 0`. `model_copy` skipping validation: the search of `src/hadavin_sdk` has many `model_copy` calls (listed them: `app.py:156`, `models.py:141`, `approvals/requests.py:124`, `:166`, `:198`, `:207`, `runtime/agent_loop.py:879`, `tools.py:494` (`spec.model_copy(deep=True)` copies a `ToolSpec`, not a Route) and the memory store), none of which copies a `Route`, so src has no Route copy; found one in the tests: `tests/test_k1b_replay.py:381-382` (`_zero_price` builds a zero-price Route with `model_copy(update=...)`, which does not run field validators), so a Route-level check would not stop a `model_copy` bypass (`Route` is frozen through `_Strict`'s `model_config` at `config.py:61`, which blocks assignment but not `model_copy`; `class Route` itself is `config.py:64`) and that test would keep passing; demos were grepped for `Route(` (`askdata/config.py:64`, `askdata/fixtures.py:24`); also grepped `src`, `tests` and `demos` for `model_copy` and found no other Route copy (see C-3 (b)). |
| C-5 | Nit | reviewer finding | DECLINE | Fixture and error-text assumptions: the codebase reviewer verified `LIVE_LLM` against AnthropicSettings (`table.py:43-61`) and the error match; the runner's tests pass on the patched tree. Cite check (src,): the reviewer's cite `config.py:453` is a blank line in this checkout (`_read_yaml` runs from `config.py:446` and ends at `:452`), so it is not the error text.
